| 64 | audit #6 (visibility_ok) visibility_ok in the point-in-time replay is true by construction. Lines of delay_v2 in step5_policy.py: d2 = dict(d); d2["available"] = d["available"] + MINUTE lag = d2["available"] - d["t"] vis_new = int(np.searchsorted(d2["available"], t + 2 * MINUTE, side="right")) vis_old = int(np.searchsorted(d2["available"], t + MINUTE, side="right")) prefix = compute(s.truncate(d2, vis_new)) replay.append(dict(row=idx, visible_rows_at_t_plus_2=vis_new, visible_rows_at_t_plus_1=vis_old, visibility_ok=(vis_new == idx + 1 and vis_old == idx), differing_outputs=off)) ok_replay = all(r["visibility_ok"] and not r["differing_outputs"] for r in replay) print(f"delay v2 {label}: values identical {80 - len(different)}/80, available_at = t+2min {len(detector) - len(wrong_lag)}/80 outputs ({finite_rows} finite values), replay {sum(r['visibility_ok'] and not r['differing_outputs'] for r in replay)}/3", flush=True) The visible-row counts come from searchsorted on d2['available'], which is built as d['available'] + one minute from the same t array, so vis_new == idx + 1 and vis_old == idx hold for any feature values. Only the comparison of the prefix features with the full run exercises the data. | section431_step3_all_in_one_results/section_4_3_closing_report_addendum_reaudit_20261031T042219Z.json | `794defeec9d1a3624c8f1c56459fb8f11ffc7251177bb5f20cd64ec88ada438c` |
